89: +
90: +func linksEndpoints(links map[int]clablinks.Link) []clablinks.Endpoint {
91: +	var endpoints []clablinks.Endpoint
92: +	for _, link := range links {
93: +		endpoints = append(endpoints, link.GetEndpoints()...)
94: +	}
95: +
96: +	return endpoints
97: +}
98: +
99: +// applyLinkImpairments sets the link impairments of the given endpoints on their interfaces.
100: +// Impairments don't survive moving an interface to another namespace, so they have to be set
101: +// again whenever the interfaces of a node are restored.
102: +func applyLinkImpairments(ctx context.Context, endpoints []clablinks.Endpoint) error {
103: +	var errs []error
104: +	moduleLoaded := false
105: +
106: +	for _, endpoint := range endpoints {
107: +		netem := endpoint.GetNetem()
108: +		if netem == nil {
109: +			continue
110: +		}
111: +
112: +		if !moduleLoaded {
113: +			loadNetemModule(ctx)
114: +			moduleLoaded = true
115: +		}
116: +
117: +		if err := applyEndpointImpairment(ctx, endpoint, netem); err != nil {
118: +			errs = append(
119: +				errs,
120: +				fmt.Errorf("failed to set the link impairment of %s: %w", endpoint, err),
121: +			)
122: +		}
123: +	}
124: +
125: +	return errors.Join(errs...)
126: +}
127: +
128: +func applyEndpointImpairment(
129: +	ctx context.Context,
130: +	endpoint clablinks.Endpoint,
131: +	netem *clablinks.Netem,
132: +) error {
133: +	node := endpoint.GetNode()
134: +
135: +	return node.ExecFunction(ctx, func(ns.NetNS) error {
136: +		nodeNS, err := ns.GetCurrentNS()
137: +		if err != nil {
138: +			return err
139: +		}
140: +		defer nodeNS.Close()
141: +
142: +		tcnl, err := clabnetem.NewTC(int(nodeNS.Fd()))
143: +		if err != nil {
144: +			return err
145: +		}
146: +		defer func() {
147: +			if err := tcnl.Close(); err != nil {
148: +				log.Debugf("could not close rtnetlink socket: %v", err)
149: +			}
150: +		}()
151: +
152: +		link, err := net.InterfaceByName(endpoint.GetIfaceName())
153: +		if err != nil {
154: +			return err
155: +		}
156: +
157: +		if err := clabnetem.ReplaceImpairments(
158: +			tcnl,
159: +			link,
160: +			netem.Delay,
161: +			netem.Jitter,
162: +			netem.Loss,
163: +			netem.Rate,
164: +			netem.Corruption,
165: +		); err != nil {
166: +			return err
167: +		}
168: +
169: +		log.Info(
170: +			"Set link impairments",
171: +			"node", node.GetShortName(),
172: +			"interface", endpoint.GetIfaceName(),
173: +		)
174: +
175: +		return nil
176: +	})
177: +}
178: +
179: +// loadNetemModule loads the sch_netem kernel module, which some distributions don't load on
180: +// their own.
181: +func loadNetemModule(ctx context.Context) {
182: +	if err := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "modprobe", "sch_netem").Run(); err != nil {
183: +		log.Debug("failed to load the sch_netem kernel module", "err", err)
184: +	}
185: +}

226: @@ -16,5 +16,47 @@ Labs are meant to be a reflection of real-world scenarios. To make simulated net
227:  These commands allow users to set, show and reset link impairments (delay, jitter, packet loss) on any link that belongs to a container node and create labs simulating real-world network conditions.
228:  
229:  ```bash title="setting packet loss at 10% rate on eth1 interface of clab-netem-r1 node"
230:  containerlab tools netem set -n clab-netem-r1 -i eth1 --loss 10
231:  ```
232: +
233: +## Impairments in the topology
234: +
235: +Impairments can also be part of the topology, so they are set whenever the lab is deployed. The `netem` key of a link endpoint takes the same impairments as `tools netem set`, which apply to the packets the endpoint's interface sends:
236: +
237: +```yaml
238: +topology:
239: +  links:
240: +    - type: veth
241: +      endpoints:
242: +        - node: r1
243: +          interface: eth1
244: +          netem:
245: +            delay: 50ms
246: +            jitter: 5ms
247: +            loss: 0.5
248: +        - node: r2
249: +          interface: eth1
250: +          netem:
251: +            rate: 10000
252: +```
253: +
254: +| Key          | Meaning                                                               |
255: +| ------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |
256: +| `delay`      | Time to delay the packets, e.g. `100ms`                               |
257: +| `jitter`     | Delay variation, e.g. `10ms`; needs a `delay`, at most `2.147483647s` |
258: +| `loss`       | Random packet loss in percent                                         |
259: +| `rate`       | Rate limit in kbit/s                                                  |
260: +| `corruption` | Random packet corruption in percent                                   |
261: +
262: +Brief links take a list with the impairment of each endpoint, in the order of the endpoints. An empty entry leaves its endpoint unimpaired:
263: +
264: +```yaml
265: +topology:
266: +  links:
267: +    - endpoints: ["r1:eth1", "r2:eth1"]
268: +      netem:
269: +        - delay: 50ms
270: +        - {}
271: +```
272: +
273: +Impairments are supported on veth links. Containerlab sets them again when a node is started or restarted, since a node's interfaces lose their impairments when it is stopped, and when the lab is reconciled with a changed topology. `tools netem` can still change them while the lab runs, until containerlab sets the topology's impairments again. Removing an impairment from the topology doesn't remove it from a running lab; use `tools netem reset` for that.

478: index 0000000..014a931
479: --- /dev/null
480: +++ b/links/netem.go
481: @@ -0,0 +1,88 @@
482: +package links
483: +
484: +import (
485: +	"fmt"
486: +	"math"
487: +	"time"
488: +)
489: +
490: +// maxNetemJitter is the highest jitter the kernel accepts without capping it.
491: +const maxNetemJitter = math.MaxInt32 * time.Nanosecond
492: +
493: +// maxNetemRate is the highest rate in kbit/s that can be converted to bytes/s.
494: +const maxNetemRate = math.MaxUint64 / 1000
495: +
496: +// NetemRaw is the link impairment of an endpoint as defined in the topology file.
497: +// It applies to the packets the endpoint's interface sends.
498: +type NetemRaw struct {
499: +	Delay      string  `yaml:"delay,omitempty"`
500: +	Jitter     string  `yaml:"jitter,omitempty"`
501: +	Loss       float64 `yaml:"loss,omitempty"`
502: +	Rate       uint64  `yaml:"rate,omitempty"`
503: +	Corruption float64 `yaml:"corruption,omitempty"`
504: +}
505: +
506: +// Netem is the resolved link impairment of an endpoint.
507: +type Netem struct {
508: +	Delay      time.Duration
509: +	Jitter     time.Duration
510: +	Loss       float64
511: +	Rate       uint64
512: +	Corruption float64
513: +}
514: +
515: +// Resolve validates the impairment and returns nil if it sets nothing.
516: +func (r *NetemRaw) Resolve() (*Netem, error) {
517: +	if r == nil {
518: +		return nil, nil
519: +	}
520: +
521: +	netem := &Netem{Loss: r.Loss, Rate: r.Rate, Corruption: r.Corruption}
522: +
523: +	var err error
524: +	if netem.Delay, err = parseNetemDuration("delay", r.Delay); err != nil {
525: +		return nil, err
526: +	}
527: +	if netem.Jitter, err = parseNetemDuration("jitter", r.Jitter); err != nil {
528: +		return nil, err
529: +	}
530: +
531: +	if netem.Jitter != 0 && netem.Delay == 0 {
532: +		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem jitter cannot be set without setting delay")
533: +	}
534: +	if netem.Jitter > maxNetemJitter {
535: +		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem jitter must be at most %s", maxNetemJitter)
536: +	}
537: +	if !(netem.Loss >= 0 && netem.Loss <= 100) {
538: +		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem loss must be in the range between 0 and 100")
539: +	}
540: +	if !(netem.Corruption >= 0 && netem.Corruption <= 100) {
541: +		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem corruption must be in the range between 0 and 100")
542: +	}
543: +
544: +	if netem.Rate > maxNetemRate {
545: +		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem rate must be at most %d kbit/s", uint64(maxNetemRate))
546: +	}
547: +
548: +	if *netem == (Netem{}) {
549: +		return nil, nil
550: +	}
551: +
552: +	return netem, nil
553: +}
554: +
555: +func parseNetemDuration(name, value string) (time.Duration, error) {
556: +	if value == "" {
557: +		return 0, nil
558: +	}
559: +
560: +	duration, err := time.ParseDuration(value)
561: +	if err != nil {
562: +		return 0, fmt.Errorf("netem %s %q is not a duration, e.g. 100ms: %w", name, value, err)
563: +	}
564: +	if duration < 0 {
565: +		return 0, fmt.Errorf("netem %s %q must not be negative", name, value)
566: +	}
567: +
568: +	return duration, nil
569: +}

588: +func TestNetemRawResolve(t *testing.T) {
589: +	tests := map[string]struct {
590: +		raw     *NetemRaw
591: +		want    *Netem
592: +		wantErr string
593: +	}{
594: +		"nothing set": {
595: +			raw: &NetemRaw{},
596: +		},
597: +		"no impairment": {
598: +			raw: nil,
599: +		},
600: +		"every impairment": {
601: +			raw: &NetemRaw{Delay: "50ms", Jitter: "5ms", Loss: 0.5, Rate: 10000, Corruption: 0.1},
602: +			want: &Netem{
603: +				Delay:      50 * time.Millisecond,
604: +				Jitter:     5 * time.Millisecond,
605: +				Loss:       0.5,
606: +				Rate:       10000,
607: +				Corruption: 0.1,
608: +			},
609: +		},
610: +		"delay that is not a duration": {
611: +			raw:     &NetemRaw{Delay: "50"},
612: +			wantErr: `netem delay "50" is not a duration`,
613: +		},
614: +		"negative jitter": {
615: +			raw:     &NetemRaw{Delay: "50ms", Jitter: "-5ms"},
616: +			wantErr: "must not be negative",
617: +		},
618: +		"jitter without delay": {
619: +			raw:     &NetemRaw{Jitter: "5ms"},
620: +			wantErr: "jitter cannot be set without setting delay",
621: +		},
622: +		"jitter the kernel would cap": {
623: +			raw:     &NetemRaw{Delay: "5m", Jitter: "1m"},
624: +			wantErr: "netem jitter must be at most 2.147483647s",
625: +		},
626: +		"loss above 100": {
627: +			raw:     &NetemRaw{Loss: 101},
628: +			wantErr: "loss must be in the range between 0 and 100",
629: +		},
630: +		"loss that is not a number": {
631: +			raw:     &NetemRaw{Loss: math.NaN()},
632: +			wantErr: "loss must be in the range between 0 and 100",
633: +		},
634: +		"rate whose bytes/s overflow": {
635: +			raw:     &NetemRaw{Rate: math.MaxUint64},
636: +			wantErr: "netem rate must be at most",
637: +		},
638: +		"negative corruption": {
639: +			raw:     &NetemRaw{Corruption: -1},
640: +			wantErr: "corruption must be in the range between 0 and 100",
641: +		},
642: +	}
643: +
644: +	for name, tc := range tests {
645: +		t.Run(name, func(t *testing.T) {
646: +			got, err := tc.raw.Resolve()
647: +			if tc.wantErr != "" {
648: +				if err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), tc.wantErr) {
649: +					t.Fatalf("want error containing %q, got %v", tc.wantErr, err)
650: +				}
651: +				return
652: +			}
653: +			if err != nil {
654: +				t.Fatal(err)
655: +			}
656: +			if d := cmp.Diff(tc.want, got); d != "" {
657: +				t.Fatalf("netem mismatch (-want +got):\n%s", d)
658: +			}
659: +		})
660: +	}
661: +}
662: +
663: +// resolveNetemLink reads a link definition and resolves it between two fake nodes.
664: +func resolveNetemLink(t *testing.T, definition string) (Link, error) {
665: +	t.Helper()
666: +
667: +	var ld LinkDefinition
668: +	if err := yaml.Unmarshal([]byte(definition), &ld); err != nil {
669: +		return nil, err
670: +	}
671: +
672: +	return ld.Link.Resolve(&ResolveParams{Nodes: map[string]Node{
673: +		"r1": newFakeNode("r1"),
674: +		"r2": newFakeNode("r2"),
675: +	}})
676: +}

678: +func TestNetemOfLinkEndpoints(t *testing.T) {
679: +	tests := map[string]string{
680: +		"brief link": `
681: +endpoints: ["r1:eth1", "r2:eth1"]
682: +netem:
683: +  - delay: 50ms
684: +    loss: 1
685: +  - {}
686: +`,
687: +		"extended link": `
688: +type: veth
689: +endpoints:
690: +  - node: r1
691: +    interface: eth1
692: +    netem:
693: +      delay: 50ms
694: +      loss: 1
695: +  - node: r2
696: +    interface: eth1
697: +`,
698: +	}
699: +
700: +	for name, definition := range tests {
701: +		t.Run(name, func(t *testing.T) {
702: +			link, err := resolveNetemLink(t, definition)
703: +			if err != nil {
704: +				t.Fatal(err)
705: +			}
706: +
707: +			endpoints := link.GetEndpoints()
708: +			want := &Netem{Delay: 50 * time.Millisecond, Loss: 1}
709: +			if d := cmp.Diff(want, endpoints[0].GetNetem()); d != "" {
710: +				t.Fatalf("netem of r1:eth1 mismatch (-want +got):\n%s", d)
711: +			}
712: +			if endpoints[1].GetNetem() != nil {
713: +				t.Fatalf("r2:eth1 must not be impaired, got %+v", endpoints[1].GetNetem())
714: +			}
715: +		})
716: +	}
717: +}

719: +func TestNetemOfLinkEndpointsIsValidated(t *testing.T) {
720: +	tests := map[string]struct {

722: +		wantErr    string
723: +	}{

725: +			definition: `

729: +`,
730: +			wantErr: "invalid netem for r1:eth1",
731: +		},
732: +		"more impairments than endpoints": {
733: +			definition: `
734: +endpoints: ["r1:eth1", "r2:eth1"]
735: +netem: [{delay: 1ms}, {delay: 1ms}, {delay: 1ms}]
736: +`,
737: +			wantErr: "number of netem entries exceeds the number of endpoints",
738: +		},
739: +		"a link that isn't a veth link": {
740: +			definition: `
741: +endpoints: ["r1:eth1", "host:r1-eth1"]
742: +netem: [{delay: 1ms}]
743: +`,
744: +			wantErr: "netem is only supported on veth links",
745: +		},
746: +	}
747: +
748: +	for name, tc := range tests {
749: +		t.Run(name, func(t *testing.T) {
750: +			_, err := resolveNetemLink(t, tc.definition)
751: +			if err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), tc.wantErr) {
752: +				t.Fatalf("want error containing %q, got %v", tc.wantErr, err)
753: +			}
754: +		})
755: +	}
756: +}

1030: +func TestNetemDurationPattern(t *testing.T) {
1031: +	b, err := os.ReadFile("clab.schema.json")
1032: +	if err != nil {
1033: +		t.Fatal(err)
1034: +	}
1035: +	var schema struct {
1036: +		Definitions map[string]struct {
1037: +			Properties map[string]struct {
1038: +				Pattern string
1039: +			}
1040: +		}
1041: +	}
1042: +	if err := json.Unmarshal(b, &schema); err != nil {
1043: +		t.Fatal(err)
1044: +	}
1045: +	pattern := regexp.MustCompile(schema.Definitions["netem"].Properties["delay"].Pattern)
1046: +	for _, tc := range []struct {
1047: +		duration string
1048: +		valid    bool
1049: +	}{
1050: +		{duration: "100ms", valid: true},
1051: +		{duration: "1.5s", valid: true},
1052: +		{duration: "1m30s", valid: true},
1053: +		{duration: "250µs", valid: true},
1054: +		{duration: "250us", valid: true},
1055: +		{duration: "100"},
1056: +		{duration: "-5ms"},
1057: +		{duration: "5 ms"},
1058: +	} {
1059: +		t.Run(tc.duration, func(t *testing.T) {
1060: +			if got := pattern.MatchString(tc.duration); got != tc.valid {
1061: +				t.Errorf("schema accepts = %v, want %v", got, tc.valid)
1062: +			}
1063: +			d, err := time.ParseDuration(tc.duration)
1064: +			if tc.valid && (err != nil || d < 0) {
1065: +				t.Errorf("the schema accepts %q, but the topology doesn't: %v", tc.duration, err)
1066: +			}
1067: +		})
1068: +	}
1069: +}

1118: +This suite tests the link impairments (netem) defined in the topology: containerlab sets them when
1119: +the lab is deployed, sets them again when a node is started or restarted, and updates them on apply.
1120: +

1128: +Suite Teardown      Run Clab Command    destroy --name ${lab-name} --cleanup
1129: +

1133: +${runtime}                  docker

1136: +${invalid-vars}             34-link-netem.vars.invalid.yml
1137: +${runtime-cli-exec-cmd}     docker exec
1138: +
1139: +
1140: +*** Test Cases ***
1141: +Deploy ${lab-name} lab
1142: +    ${rc}    ${output} =    Run Clab Command

To make simulated net\n227:  These commands allow users to set, show and reset link impairments (delay, jitter, packet loss) on any link that belongs to a container node and create labs simulating real-world network conditions.\n228:  \n229:  ```bash title=\"setting packet loss at 10% rate on eth1 interface of clab-netem-r1 node\"\n230:  containerlab tools netem set -n clab-netem-r1 -i eth1 --loss 10\n231:  ```\n232: +\n233: +## Impairments in the topology\n234: +\n235: +Impairments can also be part of the topology, so they are set whenever the lab is deployed. The `netem` key of a link endpoint takes the same impairments as `tools netem set`, which apply to the packets the endpoint's interface sends:\n236: +\n237: +```yaml\n238: +topology:\n239: +  links:\n240: +    - type: veth\n241: +      endpoints:\n242: +        - node: r1\n243: +          interface: eth1\n244: +          netem:\n245: +            delay: 50ms\n246: +            jitter: 5ms\n247: +            loss: 0.5\n248: +        - node: r2\n249: +          interface: eth1\n250: +          netem:\n251: +            rate: 10000\n252: +```\n253: +\n254: +| Key          | Meaning                                                               |\n255: +| ------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n256: +| `delay`      | Time to delay the packets, e.g. `100ms`                               |\n257: +| `jitter`     | Delay variation, e.g. `10ms`; needs a `delay`, at most `2.147483647s` |\n258: +| `loss`       | Random packet loss in percent                                         |\n259: +| `rate`       | Rate limit in kbit/s                                                  |\n260: +| `corruption` | Random packet corruption in percent                                   |\n261: +\n262: +Brief links take a list with the impairment of each endpoint, in the order of the endpoints. An empty entry leaves its endpoint unimpaired:\n263: +\n264: +```yaml\n265: +topology:\n266: +  links:\n267: +    - endpoints: [\"r1:eth1\", \"r2:eth1\"]\n268: +      netem:\n269: +        - delay: 50ms\n270: +        - {}\n271: +```\n272: +\n273: +Impairments are supported on veth links. Containerlab sets them again when a node is started or restarted, since a node's interfaces lose their impairments when it is stopped, and when the lab is reconciled with a changed topology. `tools netem` can still change them while the lab runs, until containerlab sets the topology's impairments again. To make simulated net\n These commands allow users to set, show and reset link impairments (delay, jitter, packet loss) on any link that belongs to a container node and create labs simulating real-world network conditions.\n \n ```bash title=\"setting packet loss at 10% rate on eth1 interface of clab-netem-r1 node\"\n containerlab tools netem set -n clab-netem-r1 -i eth1 --loss 10\n ```\n+\n+## Impairments in the topology\n+\n+Impairments can also be part of the topology, so they are set whenever the lab is deployed. The `netem` key of a link endpoint takes the same impairments as `tools netem set`, which apply to the packets the endpoint's interface sends:\n+\n+```yaml\n+topology:\n+  links:\n+    - type: veth\n+      endpoints:\n+        - node: r1\n+          interface: eth1\n+          netem:\n+            delay: 50ms\n+            jitter: 5ms\n+            loss: 0.5\n+        - node: r2\n+          interface: eth1\n+          netem:\n+            rate: 10000\n+```\n+\n+| Key          | Meaning                                                               |\n+| ------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n+| `delay`      | Time to delay the packets, e.g. `100ms`                               |\n+| `jitter`     | Delay variation, e.g. `10ms`; needs a `delay`, at most `2.147483647s` |\n+| `loss`       | Random packet loss in percent                                         |\n+| `rate`       | Rate limit in kbit/s                                                  |\n+| `corruption` | Random packet corruption in percent                                   |\n+\n+Brief links take a list with the impairment of each endpoint, in the order of the endpoints. An empty entry leaves its endpoint unimpaired:\n+\n+```yaml\n+topology:\n+  links:\n+    - endpoints: [\"r1:eth1\", \"r2:eth1\"]\n+      netem:\n+        - delay: 50ms\n+        - {}\n+```\n+\n+Impairments are supported on veth links. Containerlab sets them again when a node is started or restarted, since a node's interfaces lose their impairments when it is stopped, and when the lab is reconciled with a changed topology. `tools netem` can still change them while the lab runs, until containerlab sets the topology's impairments again. Removing an impairment from the topology doesn't remove it from a running lab; use `tools netem reset` for that.\ndiff --git a/docs/manual/topo-def-file.md b/docs/manual/topo-def-file.md\nindex <NodeA-IPv6-Address>          # optional e.g. 2001:db8::1/64\n+        netem: <NodeA-Impairment>           # optional, see link impairments\n       - node: <NodeB-Name>                  # mandatory\n <type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.\n\n**Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.\n\n## 1. Think Before Coding\n\n**Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**\n\nBefore implementing:\n\n- State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.\n- If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.\n- If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.\n- If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.\n\n## 2. Simplicity First\n\n**Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**\n\n- No features beyond what was asked.\n- No abstractions for single-use code.\n- No \"flexibility\" or \"configurability\" that wasn't requested.\n- No error handling for impossible scenarios.\n- If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.\n\nAsk yourself: \"Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?\" If yes, simplify.\n\n## 3. Surgical Changes\n\n**Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**\n\nWhen editing existing code:\n\n- Don't \"improve\" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.\n- Don't refactor things that aren't broken.\n- Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.\n- If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.\n\nWhen your changes create orphans:\n\n- Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.\n- Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.\n\nThe test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.\n\n## 4. Goal-Driven Execution\n\n**Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**\n\nTransform tasks into verifiable goals:\n\n- \"Add validation\" → \"Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass\"\n- \"Fix the bug\" → \"Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass\"\n- \"Refactor X\" → \"Ensure tests pass before and after\"\n\nFor multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:\n\n```\n1. [Step] → verify: [check]\n2. [Step] → verify: [check]\n3. [Step] → verify: [check]\n```\n\nStrong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria (\"make it work\") require constant clarification.\n\n---\n\n**These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.",

<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

1: package core
2: 
3: import (
4: 	"context"
5: 	"errors"
6: 	"fmt"
7: 	"net"
8: 	"os/exec"
9: 
10: 	"github.com/charmbracelet/log"
11: 	"github.com/containernetworking/plugins/pkg/ns"
12: 	clablinks "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/links"
13: 	clabnetem "github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/netem"
14: )
15: 
16: func linksEndpoints(links map[int]clablinks.Link) []clablinks.Endpoint {
17: 	var endpoints []clablinks.Endpoint
18: 	for _, link := range links {
19: 		endpoints = append(endpoints, link.GetEndpoints()...)
20: 	}
21: 
22: 	return endpoints
23: }
24: 
25: // applyLinkImpairments sets the link impairments of the given endpoints on their interfaces.
26: // Impairments don't survive moving an interface to another namespace, so they have to be set
27: // again whenever the interfaces of a node are restored.
28: func applyLinkImpairments(ctx context.Context, endpoints []clablinks.Endpoint) error {
29: 	var errs []error
30: 	moduleLoaded := false
31: 
32: 	for _, endpoint := range endpoints {
33: 		netem := endpoint.GetNetem()
34: 		if netem == nil {
35: 			continue
36: 		}
37: 
38: 		if !moduleLoaded {
39: 			loadNetemModule(ctx)
40: 			moduleLoaded = true
41: 		}
42: 
43: 		if err := applyEndpointImpairment(ctx, endpoint, netem); err != nil {
44: 			errs = append(
45: 				errs,
46: 				fmt.Errorf("failed to set the link impairment of %s: %w", endpoint, err),
47: 			)
48: 		}
49: 	}
50: 
51: 	return errors.Join(errs...)
52: }
53: 
54: func applyEndpointImpairment(
55: 	ctx context.Context,
56: 	endpoint clablinks.Endpoint,
57: 	netem *clablinks.Netem,
58: ) error {
59: 	node := endpoint.GetNode()
60: 
61: 	return node.ExecFunction(ctx, func(ns.NetNS) error {
62: 		nodeNS, err := ns.GetCurrentNS()
63: 		if err != nil {
64: 			return err
65: 		}
66: 		defer nodeNS.Close()
67: 
68: 		tcnl, err := clabnetem.NewTC(int(nodeNS.Fd()))
69: 		if err != nil {
70: 			return err
71: 		}
72: 		defer func() {
73: 			if err := tcnl.Close(); err != nil {
74: 				log.Debugf("could not close rtnetlink socket: %v", err)
75: 			}
76: 		}()
77: 
78: 		link, err := net.InterfaceByName(endpoint.GetIfaceName())
79: 		if err != nil {
80: 			return err
81: 		}
82: 
83: 		if err := clabnetem.ReplaceImpairments(
84: 			tcnl,
85: 			link,
86: 			netem.Delay,
87: 			netem.Jitter,
88: 			netem.Loss,
89: 			netem.Rate,
90: 			netem.Corruption,
91: 		); err != nil {
92: 			return err
93: 		}
94: 
95: 		log.Info(
96: 			"Set link impairments",
97: 			"node", node.GetShortName(),
98: 			"interface", endpoint.GetIfaceName(),
99: 		)
100: 
101: 		return nil
102: 	})
103: }
104: 
105: // loadNetemModule loads the sch_netem kernel module, which some distributions don't load on
106: // their own.
107: func loadNetemModule(ctx context.Context) {
108: 	if err := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "modprobe", "sch_netem").Run(); err != nil {
109: 		log.Debug("failed to load the sch_netem kernel module", "err", err)
110: 	}
111: }

9: // maxNetemJitter is the highest jitter the kernel accepts without capping it.
10: const maxNetemJitter = math.MaxInt32 * time.Nanosecond
11: 
12: // maxNetemRate is the highest rate in kbit/s that can be converted to bytes/s.
13: const maxNetemRate = math.MaxUint64 / 1000
14: 
15: // NetemRaw is the link impairment of an endpoint as defined in the topology file.
16: // It applies to the packets the endpoint's interface sends.
17: type NetemRaw struct {

19: 	Jitter     string  `yaml:"jitter,omitempty"`

22: 	Corruption float64 `yaml:"corruption,omitempty"`
23: }
24: 
25: // Netem is the resolved link impairment of an endpoint.
26: type Netem struct {
27: 	Delay      time.Duration
28: 	Jitter     time.Duration
29: 	Loss       float64
30: 	Rate       uint64
31: 	Corruption float64
32: }
33: 
34: // Resolve validates the impairment and returns nil if it sets nothing.
35: func (r *NetemRaw) Resolve() (*Netem, error) {
36: 	if r == nil {
37: 		return nil, nil
38: 	}
39:

44: 		return nil, err
45: 	}
46: 	if netem.Jitter, err = parseNetemDuration("jitter", r.Jitter); err != nil {
47: 		return nil, err
48: 	}
49: 
50: 	if netem.Jitter != 0 && netem.Delay == 0 {
51: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem jitter cannot be set without setting delay")
52: 	}
53: 	if netem.Jitter > maxNetemJitter {
54: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem jitter must be at most %s", maxNetemJitter)
55: 	}
56: 	if !(netem.Loss >= 0 && netem.Loss <= 100) {
57: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem loss must be in the range between 0 and 100")
58: 	}
59: 	if !(netem.Corruption >= 0 && netem.Corruption <= 100) {
60: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("netem corruption must be in the range between 0 and 100")
61: 	}

69: 	}

1: package netem
2: 
3: import (
4: 	"fmt"
5: 	"math"
6: 	"net"
7: 	"time"
8: 
9: 	"github.com/florianl/go-tc"
10: 	"github.com/florianl/go-tc/core"
11: 	"github.com/mdlayher/netlink"
12: 	"golang.org/x/sys/unix"
13: )
14: 
15: func init() {
16: 	// Initialize the clock parameters from /proc/net/psched.
17: 	// This is required for accurate tick-to-time conversion in the go-tc library.
18: 	_ = core.InitializeClock()
19: }
20: 
21: // NewTC returns a new tc client opened for a given network namespace.
22: // Must be closed after use.
23: func NewTC(ns int) (*tc.Tc, error) {
24: 	tcnl, err := tc.Open(&tc.Config{
25: 		NetNS: ns,
26: 	})
27: 	if err != nil {
28: 		return nil, err
29: 	}
30: 
31: 	return tcnl, nil
32: }
33: 
34: // SetImpairments sets the impairments on the given interface of a node and returns its qdisc.
35: func SetImpairments(tcnl *tc.Tc, nodeName string, link *net.Interface, delay, jitter time.Duration,
36: 	loss float64, rate uint64, probability float64,
37: ) (*tc.Object, error) {
38: 	err := ReplaceImpairments(tcnl, link, delay, jitter, loss, rate, probability)
39: 	if err != nil {
40: 		return nil, err
41: 	}
42: 
43: 	// get qdisc of an interface after we set it
44: 	qdiscs, err := tcnl.Qdisc().Get()
45: 	if err != nil {
46: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("could not get all qdiscs: %v", err)
47: 	}
48: 
49: 	for idx := range qdiscs {
50: 		if qdiscs[idx].Ifindex == uint32(link.Index) {
51: 			return &qdiscs[idx], nil
52: 		}
53: 	}
54: 
55: 	return nil, fmt.Errorf("could not find qdisc for interface %q", link.Name)
56: }
57: 
58: // ReplaceImpairments sets the impairments on the given interface, replacing the ones it has.
59: func ReplaceImpairments(tcnl *tc.Tc, link *net.Interface, delay, jitter time.Duration,
60: 	loss float64, rate uint64, probability float64,
61: ) error {
62: 	err := tcnl.SetOption(netlink.ExtendedAcknowledge, true)
63: 	if err != nil {
64: 		return fmt.Errorf("could not set option ExtendedAcknowledge: %v", err)
65: 	}
66: 
67: 	qdisc := tc.Object{
68: 		Msg: tc.Msg{
69: 			Family:  unix.AF_UNSPEC,
70: 			Ifindex: uint32(link.Index),
71: 			Handle:  core.BuildHandle(0x1, 0x0),
72: 			Parent:  tc.HandleRoot,
73: 			Info:    0,
74: 		},
75: 		Attribute: tc.Attribute{
76: 			Kind: "netem",
77: 			Netem: &tc.Netem{
78: 				Qopt: tc.NetemQopt{
79: 					Limit: 10000, // max number of packets netem can hold during delay
80: 				},
81: 			},
82: 		},
83: 	}
84: 
85: 	setDelay(&qdisc, delay, jitter)
86: 
87: 	setLoss(&qdisc, loss)
88: 
89: 	setRate(&qdisc, rate)
90: 
91: 	// Always set the corruption field (even if probability is 0) to allow resetting.
92: 	setCorruption(&qdisc, probability)
93: 
94: 	return tcnl.Qdisc().Replace(&qdisc)
95: }
96: 
97: // DeleteImpairments deletes the netem impairments from the given interface.
98: func DeleteImpairments(tcnl *tc.Tc, link *net.Interface) error {
99: 	qdisc := tc.Object{
100: 		Msg: tc.Msg{
101: 			Family:  unix.AF_UNSPEC,
102: 			Ifindex: uint32(link.Index),
103: 			Handle:  core.BuildHandle(0x1, 0x0),
104: 			Parent:  tc.HandleRoot,
105: 			Info:    0,
106: 		},
107: 		Attribute: tc.Attribute{
108: 			Kind:  "netem",
109: 			Netem: &tc.Netem{},
110: 		},
111: 	}
112: 	return tcnl.Qdisc().Delete(&qdisc)
113: }
114: 
115: // setDelay sets delay and jitter to the qdisc.
116: func setDelay(qdisc *tc.Object, delay, jitter time.Duration) {
117: 	delayNs := delay.Nanoseconds()
118: 	jitterNs := jitter.Nanoseconds()
119: 
120: 	qdisc.Attribute.Netem.Latency64 = &delayNs
121: 	qdisc.Attribute.Netem.Jitter64 = &jitterNs
122: }
123: 
124: // setLoss sets the loss to the qdisc.
125: func setLoss(qdisc *tc.Object, loss float64) {
126: 	qdisc.Attribute.Netem.Qopt.Loss = uint32(math.Round(math.MaxUint32 * (loss / float64(100))))
127: }
128: 
129: // setRate sets the rate to the qdisc.
130: // The rate is provided in kbit.
131: func setRate(qdisc *tc.Object, rate uint64) {
132: 	// convert to bytes
133: 	byteRate := rate * 1000 / 8
134: 	qdisc.Attribute.Netem.Rate = &tc.NetemRate{
135: 		Rate: uint32(min(byteRate, math.MaxUint32)),
136: 	}
137: 
138: 	if byteRate > math.MaxUint32 {
139: 		qdisc.Attribute.Netem.Rate64 = &byteRate
140: 	}
141: }
142: 
143: // setCorruption sets the corruption probability and correlation.
144: func setCorruption(qdisc *tc.Object, probability float64) {
145: 	qdisc.Netem.Corrupt = &tc.NetemCorrupt{
146: 		Probability: uint32(math.Round(math.MaxUint32 * (probability / float64(100)))),
147: 	}
148: }
149: 
150: // Impairments returns all link impairments of a node.
151: func Impairments(tcnl *tc.Tc) ([]tc.Object, error) {
152: 	qdiscs, err := tcnl.Qdisc().Get()
153: 	if err != nil {
154: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("could not get all qdiscs: %v", err)
155: 	}
156: 
157: 	return qdiscs, nil
158: }

13: func TestNetemRawResolve(t *testing.T) {
14: 	tests := map[string]struct {

27: 			want: &Netem{
28: 				Delay:      50 * time.Millisecond,
29: 				Jitter:     5 * time.Millisecond,
30: 				Loss:       0.5,
31: 				Rate:       10000,
32: 				Corruption: 0.1,
33: 			},
34: 		},
35: 		"delay that is not a duration": {
36: 			raw:     &NetemRaw{Delay: "50"},
37: 			wantErr: `netem delay "50" is not a duration`,
38: 		},

40: 			raw:     &NetemRaw{Delay: "50ms", Jitter: "-5ms"},
41: 			wantErr: "must not be negative",
42: 		},
43: 		"jitter without delay": {
44: 			raw:     &NetemRaw{Jitter: "5ms"},
45: 			wantErr: "jitter cannot be set without setting delay",
46: 		},
47: 		"jitter the kernel would cap": {
48: 			raw:     &NetemRaw{Delay: "5m", Jitter: "1m"},
49: 			wantErr: "netem jitter must be at most 2.147483647s",
50: 		},

52: 			raw:     &NetemRaw{Loss: 101},
53: 			wantErr: "loss must be in the range between 0 and 100",
54: 		},

56: 			raw:     &NetemRaw{Loss: math.NaN()},
57: 			wantErr: "loss must be in the range between 0 and 100",
58: 		},

60: 			raw:     &NetemRaw{Rate: math.MaxUint64},

73: 				if err == nil || !strings.Contains(err.Error(), tc.wantErr) {

75: 				}
76: 				return
77: 			}
78: 			if err != nil {

82: 				t.Fatalf("netem mismatch (-want +got):\n%s", d)
83: 			}
84: 		})

88: // resolveNetemLink reads a link definition and resolves it between two fake nodes.

90: 	t.Helper()

103: func TestNetemOfLinkEndpoints(t *testing.T) {
104: 	tests := map[string]string{
105: 		"brief link": `
106: endpoints: ["r1:eth1", "r2:eth1"]
107: netem:

121:     interface: eth1

123: 	}

178: 			}

183: func TestLinkVEthRawToLinkBriefRawKeepsTheNetem(t *testing.T) {

30: func applyResultFromPlan(plan *applyPlan) *ApplyResult {
31: 	result := &ApplyResult{
32: 		AddedNodes:        []string{},
33: 		DeletedNodes:      []string{},

39: 		NodeChangeReasons: map[string]string{},
40: 	}
41: 	if plan == nil {
42: 		return result
43: 	}
44: 
45: 	result.AddedNodes = sortedStringSet(plan.addedNodeSet)
46: 	result.DeletedNodes = sortedStringSet(plan.deletedNodeSet)
47: 	result.RecreatedNodes = sortedStringSet(plan.recreatedNodeSet)
48: 	result.StartedNodes = sortedStringSet(plan.startNodeSet)
49: 	result.AddedLinks = applyLinkNames(plan.addedLinks)
50: 	result.DeletedEndpoints = applyDeletedEndpointNames(plan.staleEndpoints)

52: 		plan.restartNodeSet,
53: 		plan.linkRestartNodeSet,
54: 	))

56: 	for nodeName, reason := range plan.nodeChangeReasons {
57: 		result.NodeChangeReasons[nodeName] = reason
58: 	}
59: 
60: 	return result
61: }

63: // Apply deploys a lab if it is absent, otherwise it aligns the deployed lab with the
64: // desired topology using runtime state as baseline.
65: func (c *CLab) Apply(
66: 	ctx context.Context,
67: 	options *ApplyOptions,
68: ) (*ApplyResult, error) {
69: 	if options == nil {
70: 		var err error
71: 		options, err = NewApplyOptions(0)
72: 		if err != nil {
73: 			return nil, err
74: 		}
75: 	}
76: 
77: 	if c.Config.Name == "" {
78: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("missing lab name")
79: 	}

82: 			"apply requires a topology file; provide --topo when it cannot be derived from --name",
83: 		)
84: 	}

87: 	if err != nil {

89: 	}

198: 	}

200: 	deployNodeNames := plan.deployNodeNames()
201: 	if err := c.prepareApply(
202: 		ctx,
203: 		deployNodeNames,
204: 		options.skipLabDirFileACLs,
205: 		currentNodes,
206: 	); err != nil {
207: 		return nil, err
208: 	}
209: 
210: 	if err := c.deleteApplyEndpoints(ctx, plan.staleEndpoints); err != nil {
211: 		return nil, err
212: 	}
213: 
214: 	if err := c.parkRecreatedNodes(ctx, plan); err != nil {
215: 		return nil, err
216: 	}
217: 
218: 	if err := c.deleteApplyNodes(ctx, plan); err != nil {
219: 		return nil, err
220: 	}
221: 
222: 	if err := c.deployApplyNodes(ctx, plan, options.maxWorkers); err != nil {
223: 		return nil, err
224: 	}
225: 
226: 	if err := c.restoreRecreatedNodes(ctx, plan); err != nil {
227: 		return nil, err
228: 	}
229: 
230: 	if err := c.removeApplyLinkEndpoints(ctx, plan.addedLinks); err != nil {
231: 		return nil, err
232: 	}
233: 
234: 	if err := c.deployLinks(ctx, plan.addedLinks, deployNodeNames); err != nil {
235: 		return nil, err
236: 	}
237: 
238: 	if err := c.postDeployApplyNodes(ctx, deployNodeNames, options.skipPostDeploy); err != nil {
239: 		return nil, err
240: 	}
241: 
242: 	if err := c.restartApplyNodes(ctx, plan.linkRestartNodeSet); err != nil {
243: 		return nil, err
244: 	}
245: 
246: 	if err := applyLinkImpairments(ctx, linksEndpoints(c.Links)); err != nil {
247: 		return nil, err
248: 	}
249: 
250: 	if err := c.updateRuntimeInfoForExistingNodes(ctx); err != nil {
251: 		return nil, err
252: 	}
253: 	if err := c.syncTailscaleProxy(ctx); err != nil {
254: 		return nil, err
255: 	}
256: 	if err := c.SyncMgmtHostRoutes(ctx); err != nil {
257: 		return nil, err
258: 	}
259: 
260: 	if _, err := c.finalize(ctx, options.exportTemplate, options.graph); err != nil {
261: 		return nil, err
262: 	}
263: 
264: 	return applyResultFromPlan(plan), nil
265: }
266: 
267: func (c *CLab) checkApplyTopologyDefinition(ctx context.Context) error {
268: 	params := *clablinks.NewVerifyLinkParams()
269: 	if len(c.Endpoints) > 0 {
270: 		runtime := c.globalRuntime()
271: 		if runtime == nil {
272: 			return fmt.Errorf("container runtime is not initialized")
273: 		}
274: 		config := runtime.Config()
275: 		if config.VerifyLinkParams != nil {
276: 			params = *config.VerifyLinkParams
277: 		}
278: 	}
279: 	params.AllowExistingEndpoint = true
280: 	if err := c.verifyLinks(ctx, &params); err != nil {
281: 		return err
282: 	}
283: 
284: 	if err := c.verifyRootNetNSLinks(); err != nil {
285: 		return err
286: 	}
287: 
288: 	for _, nodeName := range sortedNodeNames(c.Nodes) {
289: 		if err := c.Nodes[nodeName].CheckDeploymentConditions(ctx); err != nil {
290: 			return err
291: 		}
292: 	}
293: 
294: 	if err := c.verifyDuplicateAddresses(); err != nil {
295: 		return err
296: 	}
297: 
298: 	return c.verifyTailscaleProxy()
299: }
300: 
301: func (c *CLab) prepareApply(
302: 	ctx context.Context,
303: 	addedNodes []string,
304: 	skipLabDirFileACLs bool,
305: 	currentNodes map[string]*runtimeNodeGroup,
306: ) error {
307: 	existing := c.collectExistingManagementAddresses(currentNodes)
308: 	if err := c.prepareLabManagementNetwork(ctx, existing...); err != nil {
309: 		return err
310: 	}
311: 
312: 	if err := c.prepareDeployArtifacts(ctx, skipLabDirFileACLs); err != nil {
313: 		return err
314: 	}
315: 
316: 	for _, nodeName := range addedNodes {
317: 		if err := c.Nodes[nodeName].PullImage(ctx); err != nil {
318: 			return err
319: 		}
320: 	}
321: 
322: 	return nil
323: }
324: 
325: func (*CLab) removeApplyLinkEndpoints(ctx context.Context, links []clablinks.Link) error {
326: 	for _, link := range links {
327: 		for _, ep := range clablinks.RuntimeEndpoints(link) {
328: 			if err := clablinks.RemoveOwnedInterface(
329: 				ctx,
330: 				ep.GetNode(),
331: 				ep.GetIfaceName(),
332: 			); err != nil {
333: 				return err
334: 			}
335: 		}
336: 	}
337: 
338: 	return nil
339: }
340: 
341: // try to preserve the existing addressing instead of reallocation.
342: func (c *CLab) collectExistingManagementAddresses(
343: 	currentNodes map[string]*runtimeNodeGroup,
344: ) []clabtypes.ExistingAddress {
345: 	var existing []clabtypes.ExistingAddress
346: 	for name, group := range currentNodes {
347: 		for _, ctr := range group.containers {
348: 			if c.mgmtNetByNetwork(ctr.NetworkName).Network != ctr.NetworkName {
349: 				continue
350: 			}
351: 			for _, value := range []string{ctr.NetworkSettings.IPv4addr, ctr.NetworkSettings.IPv6addr} {
352: 				if ip, err := netip.ParseAddr(value); err == nil {
353: 					existing = append(
354: 						existing,
355: 						clabtypes.ExistingAddress{NodeName: name, ContainerID: ctr.ID, Address: ip},
356: 					)
357: 				}
358: 			}
359: 		}
360: 	}
361: 	return existing
362: }

16: // LinkVEthRaw is the raw (string) representation of a veth link as defined in the topology file.
17: type LinkVEthRaw struct {
18: 	LinkCommonParams `               yaml:",inline"`
19: 	Endpoints        []*EndpointRaw `yaml:"endpoints"`

23: // ToLinkBriefRaw converts the raw link into a LinkBriefRaw.
24: func (r *LinkVEthRaw) ToLinkBriefRaw() *LinkBriefRaw {
25: 	lc := &LinkBriefRaw{
26: 		Endpoints:        []string{},
27: 		LinkCommonParams: r.LinkCommonParams,
28: 	}
29:

31: 		lc.Endpoints = append(lc.Endpoints, fmt.Sprintf("%s:%s", e.Node, e.Iface))
32: 	}
33: 
34: 	if slices.ContainsFunc(r.Endpoints, func(e *EndpointRaw) bool { return e.Netem != nil }) {

47: // Resolve resolves the raw veth link definition into a Link interface that is implemented
48: // by a concrete LinkVEth struct.
49: // Resolving a veth link resolves its endpoints.
50: func (r *LinkVEthRaw) Resolve(params *ResolveParams) (Link, error) {
51: 	// filtered true means the link is in the filter provided by a user
52: 	// aka it should be resolved/created/deployed
53: 	filtered := isInFilter(params, r.Endpoints)
54: 	if !filtered {
55: 		return nil, nil
56: 	}
57: 
58: 	if r.fromBrief && r.briefDefaultLinkType(params) == LinkTypeVethStitch {
59: 		return NewVEthStitchedRawFromVEth(r).Resolve(params)
60: 	}
61: 
62: 	// create LinkVEth struct
63: 	l := NewLinkVEth()
64: 	l.LinkCommonParams = r.LinkCommonParams
65: 
66: 	// Normalize link vars to ensure JSON serialization compatibility
67: 	l.Vars = normalizeVars(l.Vars)
68: 
69: 	// resolve raw endpoints (epr) to endpoints (ep)
70: 	for _, epr := range r.Endpoints {
71: 		ep, err := epr.Resolve(params, l)
72: 		if err != nil {
73: 			return nil, err
74: 		}
75: 
76: 		// add endpoint to the link endpoints
77: 		l.Endpoints = append(l.Endpoints, ep)
78: 	}
79: 
80: 	// set default link mtu if MTU is unset
81: 	if l.MTU == 0 {
82: 		l.MTU = clabconstants.DefaultLinkMTU
83: 	}
84: 
85: 	return l, nil
86: }
87: 
88: // linkVEthRawFromLinkBriefRaw creates a raw veth link from a LinkBriefRaw.
89: func linkVEthRawFromLinkBriefRaw(lb *LinkBriefRaw) (*LinkVEthRaw, error) {
90: 	host, hostIf, node, nodeIf, err := extractHostNodeInterfaceData(lb, 0)
91: 	if err != nil {
92: 		return nil, err
93: 	}
94: 
95: 	link := &LinkVEthRaw{
96: 		LinkCommonParams: lb.LinkCommonParams,
97: 		Endpoints: []*EndpointRaw{
98: 			NewEndpointRaw(host, hostIf, ""),
99: 			NewEndpointRaw(node, nodeIf, ""),
100: 		},
101: 	}
102: 
103: 	// populate vars
104: 	if err := mapBriefVarsToEndpoints(lb, link.Endpoints); err != nil {
105: 		return nil, err
106: 	}
107: 
108: 	if len(lb.Netem) > len(link.Endpoints) {
109: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("number of netem entries exceeds the number of endpoints")
110: 	}
111: 	for i, netem := range lb.Netem {
112: 		link.Endpoints[i].Netem = netem
113: 	}
114: 
115: 	// set default link mtu if MTU is unset
116: 	if link.MTU == 0 {
117: 		link.MTU = clabconstants.DefaultLinkMTU
118: 	}
119: 
120: 	return link, nil
121: }

123: func (r *LinkVEthRaw) cleanupFilteredLink(
124: 	ctx context.Context,

126: 	nodesFilter []string,
127: ) error {

129: 		return nil
130: 	}

132: 	// probe every brief veth because its node-selected default is
133: 	// unavailable after filtering; non-stitched probes are harmless no-ops.
134: 	return cleanupFilteredVethStitch(ctx, labName, nodesFilter, r.Endpoints)
135: }

137: // defaultLinkTypeProvider is a type that can provide the default link type for a node. Currently
138: // only needed for SR-SIM nodes that use veth-stitch for a regular veth link.
139: type defaultLinkTypeProvider interface {
140: 	DefaultLinkType() LinkType
141: }
142: 
143: func (r *LinkVEthRaw) briefDefaultLinkType(params *ResolveParams) LinkType {
144: 	for _, ep := range r.Endpoints {
145: 		n, ok := params.Nodes[ep.Node]
146: 		if !ok {
147: 			continue
148: 		}
149: 
150: 		provider, ok := n.(defaultLinkTypeProvider)
151: 		if !ok {
152: 			continue
153: 		}
154: 
155: 		lt := provider.DefaultLinkType()
156: 		if lt != "" && lt != LinkTypeVEth {
157: 			return lt
158: 		}
159: 	}
160: 
161: 	return LinkTypeVEth
162: }

165: 	LinkCommonParams
166: 	Endpoints []Endpoint
167: 
168: 	deployMutex sync.Mutex
169: }

174: 	}

178: 	return LinkTypeVEth

181: func (l *LinkVEth) deployAEnd(ctx context.Context, idx int) error {
182: 	ep := l.Endpoints[idx]
183: 	// the peer Endpoint is the other of the two endpoints in the
184: 	// Endpoints slice. So do a +1 on the index and modulo operation
185: 	// to take care of the wrap around.
186: 	peerIdx := (idx + 1) % 2
187: 	peerEp := l.Endpoints[peerIdx]
188: 
189: 	log.Debugf("Creating Endpoint: %s ( --> %s )", ep, peerEp)
190: 
191: 	// build the netlink.Veth struct for the link provisioning
192: 	linkA := &netlink.Veth{
193: 		LinkAttrs: netlink.LinkAttrs{
194: 			Name: ep.GetRandIfaceName(),
195: 			MTU:  l.MTU,
196: 			// Mac address is set later on
197: 		},
198: 		PeerName: peerEp.GetRandIfaceName(),
199: 		// PeerMac address is set later on
200: 	}
201: 
202: 	// add the link
203: 	err := netlink.LinkAdd(linkA)
204: 	if err != nil {
205: 		return err
206: 	}
207: 
208: 	// cleanup is a helper that removes the veth pair if a subsequent step fails
209: 	// after LinkAdd succeeded, preventing orphaned interfaces from blocking retries.
210: 	cleanup := func(err error) error {
211: 		if delErr := netlink.LinkDel(linkA); delErr != nil {
212: 			log.Debugf(
213: 				"failed to cleanup veth pair %s after error: %v",
214: 				ep.GetRandIfaceName(),
215: 				delErr,
216: 			)
217: 		}
218: 		return err
219: 	}
220: 
221: 	// disable TXOffloading
222: 	if err := clabutils.EthtoolTXOff(ep.GetRandIfaceName()); err != nil {
223: 		return cleanup(err)
224: 	}
225: 
226: 	// the link needs to be moved to the relevant network namespace
227: 	// and enabled (up). This is done via linkSetupFunc.
228: 	// based on the endpoint type the link setup function is different.
229: 	// linkSetupFunc is executed in a netns of a node.
230: 	// if the node is a regular namespace node
231: 	// add link to node, rename, set mac and Up
232: 	err = ep.GetNode().AddLinkToContainer(ctx, linkA,
233: 		SetNameMACAndUpInterface(linkA, ep))
234: 	if err != nil {
235: 		return cleanup(err)
236: 	}
237: 
238: 	l.DeploymentState = LinkDeploymentStateHalfDeployed
239: 
240: 	// e.g. host endpoints are nodeless, and therefore the B end of the veth link should
241: 	// be deployed right after the A end is deployed.
242: 	if peerEp.IsNodeless() {
243: 		return l.deployBEnd(ctx, peerIdx)
244: 	}
245: 
246: 	return nil
247: }
248: 
249: func (l *LinkVEth) deployBEnd(ctx context.Context, idx int) error {
250: 	ep := l.Endpoints[idx]
251: 	peerEp := l.Endpoints[(idx+1)%2]
252: 
253: 	log.Debugf("Assigning Endpoint: %s ( --> %s )", ep, peerEp)
254: 
255: 	// retrieve the netlink.Link for the provided Endpoint
256: 	link, err := netlink.LinkByName(ep.GetRandIfaceName())
257: 	if err != nil {
258: 		return err
259: 	}
260: 
261: 	// disable TXOffloading
262: 	if err := clabutils.EthtoolTXOff(ep.GetRandIfaceName()); err != nil {
263: 		return err
264: 	}
265: 
266: 	// Host endpoints already live in the current namespace; set them up in place.
267: 	// Other nodeless endpoints, such as the management bridge, still need their
268: 	// node-specific setup.
269: 	if ep.IsNodeless() && ep.GetNode().GetLinkEndpointType() == LinkEndpointTypeHost {
270: 		if err := SetNameMACAndUpInterface(link, ep)(nil); err != nil {
271: 			return err
272: 		}
273: 	} else if err = ep.GetNode().AddLinkToContainer(ctx, link,
274: 		SetNameMACAndUpInterface(link, ep)); err != nil {
275: 		return err
276: 	}
277: 
278: 	l.DeploymentState = LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed
279: 
280: 	if len(l.Endpoints) == 2 {
281: 		log.Infof("Created link: %s ▪┄┄▪ %s", l.Endpoints[0], l.Endpoints[1])
282: 	}
283: 
284: 	return nil
285: }
286: 
287: // getEndpointIndex returns the index of the ep endpoint belonging to l link.
288: // An error is returned when the ep is not part of the l's endpoints.
289: func (l *LinkVEth) getEndpointIndex(ep Endpoint) (int, error) {
290: 	for idx, e := range l.Endpoints {
291: 		if e == ep {
292: 			return idx, nil
293: 		}
294: 	}
295: 
296: 	// if the endpoint is not part of the link
297: 	// build a string list of endpoints and return a meaningful error
298: 	var epStrings []string
299: 	for _, e := range l.Endpoints {
300: 		epStrings = append(epStrings, e.String())
301: 	}
302: 
303: 	return -1, fmt.Errorf(
304: 		"endpoint %s does not belong to link [ %s ]",
305: 		ep.String(),
306: 		strings.Join(epStrings, ", "),
307: 	)
308: }
309: 
310: // Deploy deploys the veth link by creating the A and B sides of the veth pair independently
311: // based on the calling endpoint.
312: func (l *LinkVEth) Deploy(ctx context.Context, ep Endpoint) error {
313: 	// since each node calls deploy on its links, we need to make sure that we only deploy
314: 	// the link once, even if multiple nodes call deploy on the same link.
315: 	l.deployMutex.Lock()
316: 	defer l.deployMutex.Unlock()
317: 
318: 	// first we need to check that the provided ep is part of this link
319: 	idx, err := l.getEndpointIndex(ep)
320: 	if err != nil {
321: 		return err
322: 	}
323: 
324: 	// Retry transient netlink errors that can occur during concurrent link operations.
325: 	// deployAEnd cleans up partial state on failure, making each retry idempotent.
326: 	return retryTransientNetlink(ctx, fmt.Sprintf("deploy link %s", ep), func() error {
327: 		switch l.DeploymentState {
328: 		case LinkDeploymentStateHalfDeployed:
329: 			return l.deployBEnd(ctx, idx)
330: 		case LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed:
331: 			return nil
332: 		default:
333: 			return l.deployAEnd(ctx, idx)
334: 		}
335: 	})
336: }
337: 
338: func (*LinkVEth) PostDeploy(context.Context) error {
339: 	return nil
340: }
341: 
342: func (l *LinkVEth) Remove(ctx context.Context) error {
343: 	l.deployMutex.Lock()
344: 	defer l.deployMutex.Unlock()
345: 	if l.DeploymentState == LinkDeploymentStateRemoved {
346: 		return nil
347: 	}
348: 	for _, ep := range l.GetEndpoints() {
349: 		err := ep.Remove(ctx)
350: 		if err != nil {
351: 			log.Debug(err)
352: 		}
353: 	}
354: 	l.DeploymentState = LinkDeploymentStateRemoved
355: 	return nil
356: }
357: 
358: func (l *LinkVEth) GetEndpoints() []Endpoint {
359: 	return l.Endpoints
360: }
361: 
362: func (l *LinkVEth) GetRuntimeEndpoints() []Endpoint {
363: 	return l.GetEndpoints()
364: }

Reasoning
Interesting: `linkVEthRawFromLinkBriefRaw` builds endpoints via `NewEndpointRaw(host, hostIf, "")` and `NewEndpointRaw(node, nodeIf, "")` — but wait, for veth brief links, endpoints[0] may be a host endpoint? Actually `extractHostNodeInterfaceData(lb, 0)`. Hmm, but if the brief link is a host link, that goes elsewhere. For a brief veth link, one endpoint may be "host:..."? Actually the check in `link_brief.go` rejects netem on host links. But wait — `linkVEthRawFromLinkBriefRaw` can be reached from LinkBriefRaw only when the parsed type is veth. But hold on — the netem check in `ToTypeSpecificRawLink` returns error for MacVLan/MgmtNet/Host. But what about `veth-stitch`? The test "a link that isn't a veth link" uses "host:r1-eth1" — matches LinkTypeHost. What about veth-stitch brief links (`LinkTypeVethStitch`)? netem would pass the check and end up in LinkVEthStitchedRaw? Let me check link_brief.go fully and see how brief parses veth-stitch. Actually, the check happens in ToTypeSpecificRawLink — the switch on lt. For veth-stitch, lt would be LinkTypeVethStitch presumably, and `vethStitchLinkFromBrief` is called. Would netem be silently dropped there? Let's check link_brief.go.

Also, note the check in link_brief.go is performed inside the loop, only for the first endpoint's node ("r1" from "r1:eth1"?). Actually let's read link_brief.go.

18: // LinkBrief is only used to have a short version of a link definition in the topology file,

20: func (l *LinkBriefRaw) ToTypeSpecificRawLink() (RawLink, error) {
21: 	// check two endpoints defined
22: 	if len(l.Endpoints) != 2 {
23: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf(
24: 			"endpoint definition should consist of exactly 2 entries. %d provided",
25: 			len(l.Endpoints),
26: 		)
27: 	}

31:

33: 		if err != nil {
34: 			continue
35: 		}
36:

38: 		if len(l.Netem) > 0 && slices.Contains(specialLinkTypes, lt) {

40: 		}

1: package links
2: 
3: import (
4: 	"fmt"
5: 	"net"

10: )
11: 
12: // EndpointRaw is the raw (string) representation of an endpoint as defined in the topology file
13: // for a given link definition.
14: type EndpointRaw struct {
15: 	Node  string         `yaml:"node"`
16: 	Iface string         `yaml:"interface"`
17: 	MAC   string         `yaml:"mac,omitempty"`
18: 	IPv4  string         `yaml:"ipv4,omitempty"`
19: 	IPv6  string         `yaml:"ipv6,omitempty"`
20: 	Vars  map[string]any `yaml:"vars,omitempty"`
21: 	Netem *NetemRaw      `yaml:"netem,omitempty"`
22: }
23: 
24: // NewEndpointRaw creates a new EndpointRaw struct.
25: func NewEndpointRaw(node, nodeIf, mac string) *EndpointRaw {
26: 	return &EndpointRaw{
27: 		Node:  node,
28: 		Iface: nodeIf,
29: 		MAC:   mac,
30: 	}
31: }
32: 
33: // Resolve resolves the EndpointRaw into an Endpoint interface that is implemented
34: // by a concrete endpoint struct such as EndpointBridge, EndpointHost, EndpointVeth.
35: // The type of an endpoint is determined by the node it belongs to.
36: // Resolving a raw endpoint adds an associated Link and Node to the endpoint.
37: // It also adds the endpoint to the node.
38: func (er *EndpointRaw) Resolve(params *ResolveParams, l Link) (Endpoint, error) {
39: 	// check if the referenced node does exist
40: 	node, exists := params.Nodes[er.Node]
41: 	if !exists {
42: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("unable to find node %s", er.Node)
43: 	}
44: 
45: 	genericEndpoint := NewEndpointGeneric(node, er.Iface, l)
46: 
47: 	if er.IPv4 != "" {
48: 		p, err := netip.ParsePrefix(er.IPv4)
49: 		if err != nil || !p.Addr().Is4() {
50: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf(
51: 				"invalid ipv4 address %q for %s:%s",
52: 				er.IPv4,
53: 				er.Node,
54: 				er.Iface,
55: 			)
56: 		}
57: 		genericEndpoint.IPv4 = p
58: 	}
59: 
60: 	if er.IPv6 != "" {
61: 		p, err := netip.ParsePrefix(er.IPv6)
62: 		if err != nil || !p.Addr().Is6() {
63: 			return nil, fmt.Errorf(
64: 				"invalid ipv6 address %q for %s:%s",
65: 				er.IPv6,
66: 				er.Node,
67: 				er.Iface,
68: 			)
69: 		}
70: 		genericEndpoint.IPv6 = p
71: 	}
72: 
73: 	netem, err := er.Netem.Resolve()
74: 	if err != nil {
75: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("invalid netem for %s:%s: %w", er.Node, er.Iface, err)
76: 	}
77: 	genericEndpoint.Netem = netem
78: 
79: 	// Normalize vars to ensure JSON serialization compatibility
80: 	// (converts map[interface{}]interface{} to map[string]any)
81: 	genericEndpoint.Vars = normalizeVars(er.Vars)
82: 
83: 	if er.MAC == "" {
84: 		// if mac is not present generate one
85: 		genericEndpoint.MAC, err = clabutils.GenMac(clabconstants.ClabOUI)
86: 		if err != nil {
87: 			return nil, err
88: 		}
89: 	} else {
90: 		// if MAC is present, set it
91: 		m, err := net.ParseMAC(er.MAC)
92: 		if err != nil {
93: 			return nil, err
94: 		}
95: 		genericEndpoint.MAC = m
96: 	}
97: 
98: 	var e Endpoint
99: 
100: 	switch node.GetLinkEndpointType() {
101: 	case LinkEndpointTypeBridge, LinkEndpointTypeBridgeNS:
102: 		e = NewEndpointBridge(genericEndpoint, false)
103: 
104: 	case LinkEndpointTypeHost:
105: 		e = NewEndpointHost(genericEndpoint)
106: 
107: 	case LinkEndpointTypeVeth:
108: 		e = NewEndpointVeth(genericEndpoint)
109: 	}
110: 	if l.GetType() == LinkTypeDummy {
111: 		e = NewEndpointDummy(genericEndpoint)
112: 	}
113: 	// also add the endpoint to the node
114: 	err = node.AddEndpoint(e)
115: 	if err != nil {
116: 		return nil, err
117: 	}
118: 
119: 	return e, nil
120: }

50: 		Endpoint:         NewEndpointRaw(node, nodeIf, ""),
51: 	}
52: 
53: 	// set default link mtu if MTU is unset
54: 	if link.MTU == 0 {
55: 		link.MTU = clabconstants.DefaultLinkMTU
56: 	}
57: 
58: 	return link, nil
59: }
60: 
61: func (r *LinkMacVlanRaw) Resolve(params *ResolveParams) (Link, error) {
62: 	var err error
63: 	// filtered true means the link is in the filter provided by a user
64: 	// aka it should be resolved/created/deployed
65: 	filtered := isInFilter(params, []*EndpointRaw{r.Endpoint})
66: 	if !filtered {
67: 		return nil, nil
68: 	}
69: 
70: 	// create the MacVlan Link
71: 	link := &LinkMacVlan{
72: 		LinkCommonParams: r.LinkCommonParams,
73: 	}
74: 
75: 	// Normalize link vars to ensure JSON serialization compatibility
76: 	link.Vars = normalizeVars(link.Vars)
77: 
78: 	// create the host side MacVlan Endpoint
79: 	link.HostEndpoint = NewEndpointMacVlan(
80: 		NewEndpointGeneric(GetHostLinkNode(), r.HostInterface, link),
81: 	)

85: 	if err != nil {
86: 		return nil, err
87: 	}
88: 	link.HostEndpoint.MAC = hostLink.Attrs().HardwareAddr
89: 
90: 	// parse the MacVlanMode
91: 	mode, err := MacVlanModeParse(r.Mode)
92: 	if err != nil {
93: 		return nil, err
94: 	}
95: 	// set the mode in the link struct
96: 	link.Mode = mode
97: 	// resolve the endpoint
98: 	link.NodeEndpoint, err = r.Endpoint.Resolve(params, link)
99: 	if err != nil {
100: 		return nil, err
101: 	}
102: 
103: 	// propagate the parent interface MTU to the link
104: 	// because the macvlan interface MTU is inherited from
105: 	// its parent interface
106: 	link.MTU, err = link.GetParentInterfaceMTU()
107: 	if err != nil {
108: 		return nil, err
109: 	}
110: 
111: 	return link, nil
112: }

136: 	if err != nil {
137: 		return err

71: // LinkVEthStitched is the resolved veth-stitch link: two veth segments
72: // (nodeA<->root-ns, nodeB<->root-ns) whose root-ns ends are joined by a tc stitch.
73: type LinkVEthStitched struct {
74: 	LinkCommonParams
75: 
76: 	lifecycleMutex sync.Mutex
77: 
78: 	// since the stitch veth lives in the root ns, we need to ensure altnames
79: 	// are per-lab unique.
80: 	labName string
81: 
82: 	segA *LinkVEth
83: 	segB *LinkVEth
84: 
85: 	// the two root-ns endpoints that get stitched together
86: 	epA Endpoint
87: 	epB Endpoint
88: }
89: 
90: func (*LinkVEthStitched) GetType() LinkType {
91: 	return LinkTypeVethStitch
92: }
93: 
94: func (l *LinkVEthStitched) GetEndpoints() []Endpoint {
95: 	return []Endpoint{l.segA.Endpoints[0], l.segB.Endpoints[0]}
96: }
97: 
98: // GetRuntimeEndpoints returns every material endpoint managed by the stitched link.
99: func (l *LinkVEthStitched) GetRuntimeEndpoints() []Endpoint {
100: 	return []Endpoint{l.segA.Endpoints[0], l.segB.Endpoints[0], l.epA, l.epB}
101: }
102: 
103: // Deploy creates the veth segment belonging to ep.
104: func (l *LinkVEthStitched) Deploy(ctx context.Context, ep Endpoint) error {
105: 	l.lifecycleMutex.Lock()
106: 	defer l.lifecycleMutex.Unlock()
107: 
108: 	if l.DeploymentState == LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed {
109: 		return nil
110: 	}
111: 
112: 	var err error
113: 	switch ep {
114: 	case l.segA.Endpoints[0]:
115: 		err = l.segA.Deploy(ctx, ep)
116: 	case l.segB.Endpoints[0]:
117: 		err = l.segB.Deploy(ctx, ep)
118: 	default:
119: 		return fmt.Errorf("endpoint %s does not belong to link [ %s, %s ]",
120: 			ep, l.segA.Endpoints[0], l.segB.Endpoints[0])
121: 	}
122: 	if err != nil {
123: 		return err
124: 	}
125: 
126: 	l.DeploymentState = LinkDeploymentStateHalfDeployed
127: 
128: 	if l.segA.DeploymentState == LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed &&
129: 		l.segB.DeploymentState == LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed {
130: 		return l.stitchSegments(ctx)
131: 	}
132: 
133: 	return nil
134: }
135: 
136: // PostDeploy tags the two root-ns far ends and joins them with tc.
137: // if they weren't already stitched in Deploy.
138: func (l *LinkVEthStitched) PostDeploy(ctx context.Context) error {
139: 	l.lifecycleMutex.Lock()
140: 	defer l.lifecycleMutex.Unlock()
141: 
142: 	if l.DeploymentState == LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed {
143: 		return nil
144: 	}
145: 	if l.segA.DeploymentState != LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed ||
146: 		l.segB.DeploymentState != LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed {
147: 		return fmt.Errorf("cannot stitch veth segments before both are fully deployed")
148: 	}
149: 
150: 	return l.stitchSegments(ctx)
151: }
152: 
153: func (l *LinkVEthStitched) stitchSegments(ctx context.Context) error {
154: 	steps := []func() error{
155: 		func() error { return markFarEnd(l.epB, l.segA.Endpoints[0], l.labName) },
156: 		func() error { return markFarEnd(l.epA, l.segB.Endpoints[0], l.labName) },
157: 		func() error { return stitch(l.epA, l.epB) },
158: 		func() error { return stitch(l.epB, l.epA) },
159: 	}
160: 	for _, step := range steps {
161: 		if err := retryTransientNetlink(ctx, "veth-stitch", step); err != nil {
162: 			return err
163: 		}
164: 	}
165: 
166: 	l.DeploymentState = LinkDeploymentStateFullDeployed
167: 
168: 	return nil
169: }
170: 
171: func (l *LinkVEthStitched) Remove(ctx context.Context) error {
172: 	l.lifecycleMutex.Lock()
173: 	defer l.lifecycleMutex.Unlock()
174: 
175: 	if l.DeploymentState == LinkDeploymentStateRemoved {
176: 		return nil
177: 	}
178: 
179: 	err := errors.Join(l.segA.Remove(ctx), l.segB.Remove(ctx))
180: 
181: 	l.DeploymentState = LinkDeploymentStateRemoved
182: 
183: 	return err
184: }
185: 
186: // buildVEthStitchSegment builds one veth segment: the real node endpoint plus a
187: // root-namespace far end named with a hash.
188: func buildVEthStitchSegment(
189: 	params *ResolveParams,
190: 	owner *LinkVEthStitched,
191: 	nodeEpRaw *EndpointRaw,
192: 	mtu int,
193: ) (*LinkVEth, Endpoint, error) {
194: 	seg := NewLinkVEth()
195: 	seg.MTU = mtu
196: 
197: 	nodeEp, err := nodeEpRaw.Resolve(params, owner)
198: 	if err != nil {
199: 		return nil, nil, err
200: 	}
201: 
202: 	// the far end sits in the root namespace (host node) with a short hash name
203: 	// that fits the 15 char name limit.
204: 	farName := stitchFarEndName(params.LabName, nodeEpRaw.Node, nodeEpRaw.Iface)
205: 	farGeneric := NewEndpointGeneric(GetHostLinkNode(), farName, seg)
206: 	farGeneric.MAC = nodeEp.GetMac()
207: 	farEp := NewEndpointHost(farGeneric)
208: 	seg.Endpoints = []Endpoint{nodeEp, farEp}
209: 
210: 	return seg, farEp, nil
211: }
212: 
213: func stitchFarEndName(labName, node, iface string) string {
214: 	sum := sha1.Sum([]byte(labName + "\x00" + node + "\x00" + iface))
215: 	return "clab-s-" + hex.EncodeToString(sum[:4]) // 7 + 8 = 15 chars
216: }
217: 
218: func markFarEnd(farEp, nodeEp Endpoint, labName string) error {
219: 	l, err := netlink.LinkByName(farEp.GetIfaceName())
220: 	if err != nil {
221: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to look up veth-stitch far end %q: %w", farEp.GetIfaceName(), err)
222: 	}
223: 
224: 	iface := nodeEp.GetIfaceAlias()
225: 	if iface == "" {
226: 		iface = nodeEp.GetIfaceName()
227: 	}
228: 
229: 	altName := StitchAltName(labName, nodeEp.GetNode().GetShortName(), iface)
230: 	if err := netlink.LinkAddAltName(l, altName); err != nil && !isAltNameNotSupportedErr(err) {
231: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to add veth-stitch altname %q: %w", altName, err)
232: 	}
233: 
234: 	return nil
235: }
236: 
237: func validateVEthStitched(r *LinkVEthStitchedRaw) error {
238: 	if len(r.Endpoints) != 2 {
239: 		return fmt.Errorf(
240: 			"veth-stitch links require exactly two endpoints, got %d",
241: 			len(r.Endpoints),
242: 		)
243: 	}
244: 
245: 	return nil
246: }
247: 
248: func (r *LinkVEthStitchedRaw) cleanupFilteredLink(
249: 	ctx context.Context,
250: 	labName string,
251: 	nodesFilter []string,
252: ) error {
253: 	return cleanupFilteredVethStitch(ctx, labName, nodesFilter, r.Endpoints)
254: }
255: 
256: func cleanupFilteredVethStitch(
257: 	ctx context.Context,
258: 	labName string,
259: 	nodesFilter []string,
260: 	endpoints []*EndpointRaw,
261: ) error {
262: 	for _, altName := range filteredVethStitchAltNames(labName, nodesFilter, endpoints) {
263: 		if err := RemoveOwnedInterface(ctx, GetHostLinkNode(), altName); err != nil {
264: 			return fmt.Errorf("failed cleaning up veth-stitch interface %q: %w", altName, err)
265: 		}
266: 	}
267: 
268: 	return nil
269: }
270: 
271: func filteredVethStitchAltNames(
272: 	labName string,
273: 	nodesFilter []string,
274: 	endpoints []*EndpointRaw,
275: ) []string {
276: 	names := make(map[string]struct{})
277: 	for _, ep := range endpoints {
278: 		if ep == nil || ep.Node == "" || ep.Iface == "" ||
279: 			!slices.Contains(nodesFilter, ep.Node) {
280: 			continue
281: 		}
282: 		names[StitchAltName(labName, ep.Node, ep.Iface)] = struct{}{}
283: 	}
284: 
285: 	result := make([]string, 0, len(names))
286: 	for name := range names {
287: 		result = append(result, name)
288: 	}
289: 	sort.Strings(result)
290: 
291: 	return result
292: }
293: 
294: // StitchAltName returns a unique name for veth-stitch links.
295: func StitchAltName(labName, node, iface string) string {
296: 	return "clab-stitch-" + clabutils.SanitizeInterfaceName(labName+"-"+node+"-"+iface)
297: }
298: 
299: // ToolsInterface returns the host-netns interface veth-stitch (and similar
300: // kinds) publish for node:iface under StitchAltName, or (nil, false) if none.
301: // Single seam for reporting/impairing the host side of the stitch.
302: func ToolsInterface(labName, node, iface string) (netlink.Link, bool) {
303: 	if labName == "" || node == "" || iface == "" {
304: 		return nil, false
305: 	}
306: 
307: 	link, err := netlink.LinkByName(StitchAltName(labName, node, iface))
308: 	if err != nil {
309: 		return nil, false
310: 	}
311: 
312: 	return link, true

StitchAltName(labName, node, iface string) string {\n\treturn \"clab-stitch-\" + clabutils.SanitizeInterfaceName(labName+\"-\"+node+\"-\"+iface)\n}\n\n// ToolsInterface returns the host-netns interface veth-stitch (and similar\n// kinds) publish for node:iface under StitchAltName, or (nil, false) if none.\n// Single seam for reporting/impairing the host side of the stitch.\nfunc Reasoning
Key finding: veth-stitch links. When a brief veth link's node type defaults to veth-stitch (SR-SIM), the LinkVEthRaw.Resolve redirects to the stitched raw, which calls `nodeEpRaw.Resolve(params, owner)` — that resolution goes through `EndpointRaw.Resolve`, which sets Netem on the endpoint. So the netem would apply to the node endpoint of the stitched link. That's actually... fine? Since the node endpoint gets the netem. Actually it would still work since applyLinkImpairments only looks at `link.GetEndpoints()` — for LinkVEthStitched, GetEndpoints returns segA.Endpoints[0] and segB.Endpoints[0], the node endpoints. And netem would be applied to the node interfaces. OK — but wait, in `LinkVEthRaw.Resolve`, the redirect happens BEFORE the endpoints are resolved... but netem lives in EndpointRaw which is passed through. So it works. Fine.

Now — extended macvlan links: `LinkMacVlanRaw` has `Endpoint *EndpointRaw` with the same `EndpointRaw` struct that now has `Netem`. Its Resolve calls `r.Endpoint.Resolve(params, link)` → netem gets set on the NodeEndpoint. And `GetEndpoints()` of LinkMacVlan returns? Let me check — likely `[HostEndpoint, NodeEndpoint]`. Then applyLinkImpairments would apply netem to the macvlan endpoint (and the host endpoint if it had netem — the host endpoint has Netem nil since it's constructed directly). So a user could specify netem on a macvlan endpoint in extended form (schema allows since endpoint definition is shared) and it would actually apply, contradicting the doc "Impairments are supported on veth links" — or is the doc right and the code silently applies it to macvlan? Actually applying netem on a macvlan interface would work technically. Hmm, but does the schema restrict netem to veth-type extended links? Let me check the schema: is `netem` added to the generic endpoint definition or per-link-type definitions? From the diff, it was added to a definition with "node"/"interface" required. Let me check the schema to see if endpoint definitions differ per link type.

Also `LinkHostRaw`: host links. And `LinkMgmtNetRaw`. For brief links the check rejects netem on host/macvlan/mgmt-net, but the extended forms (type: host, type: macvlan, type: mgmt-net) still allow netem per the schema and would set it on endpoints (host link's endpoint is nodeless? host link has one endpoint which is nodeless-ish? Let me check LinkHostRaw).

Actually, wait. The important question for correctness: what happens when applyLinkImpairments processes a host endpoint with netem? `node := endpoint.GetNode()` — the host link node is `GetHostLinkNode()` — a generic node in the root ns. ExecFunction on it — let me check its implementation.

Also mgmt-net links: endpoint type bridge — bridge endpoint's node is the mgmt bridge pseudo-node; ExecFunction on it?

Let me check `GetHostLinkNode` and its ExecFunction, and the endpoint definitions in the schema.

20: 	link netlink.Link,
21: 	f func(ns.NetNS) error,
22: ) error {
23: 	// retrieve the namespace handle

26: 		return err
27: 	}

35: 
36: func (g *GenericLinkNode) ExecFunction(_ context.Context, f func(ns.NetNS) error) error {
37: 	// retrieve the namespace handle
38: 	netns, err := ns.GetNS(g.nspath)
39: 	if err != nil {
40: 		return err
41: 	}
42: 	// execute the given function
43: 	return netns.Do(f)
44: }
45: 
46: func (g *GenericLinkNode) AddEndpoint(e Endpoint) error {
47: 	g.endpoints = append(g.endpoints, e)
48: 	return nil
49: }
50: 
51: func (g *GenericLinkNode) AdoptEndpoint(e Endpoint) error {
52: 	if e == nil {
53: 		return fmt.Errorf("node %q cannot adopt a nil endpoint", g.shortname)
54: 	}
55: 
56: 	owner := e.GetNode()
57: 	if owner == nil {
58: 		return fmt.Errorf(
59: 			"node %q cannot adopt endpoint %q without an owner",
60: 			g.shortname,
61: 			e.GetIfaceName(),
62: 		)
63: 	}
64: 
65: 	if owner.GetShortName() != g.shortname {
66: 		return fmt.Errorf(
67: 			"node %q cannot adopt endpoint %q owned by %q",
68: 			g.shortname,
69: 			e.GetIfaceName(),
70: 			owner.GetShortName(),
71: 		)
72: 	}
73: 
74: 	for _, owned := range g.endpoints {
75: 		if owned == e {
76: 			return nil
77: 		}
78: 		if owned.GetIfaceName() == e.GetIfaceName() {
79: 			return fmt.Errorf("node %q already tracks interface %q", g.shortname, e.GetIfaceName())
80: 		}
81: 	}
82: 
83: 	g.endpoints = append(g.endpoints, e)
84: 	return nil
85: }
86: 
87: func (g *GenericLinkNode) ReleaseEndpoint(e Endpoint) error {
88: 	for i, ep := range g.endpoints {
89: 		if ep != e {
90: 			continue
91: 		}
92: 
93: 		g.endpoints = append(g.endpoints[:i], g.endpoints[i+1:]...)
94: 		return nil
95: 	}
96: 
97: 	return fmt.Errorf("node %q does not own endpoint %q", g.shortname, e.GetIfaceName())
98: }
99: 
100: func (g *GenericLinkNode) GetShortName() string {
101: 	return g.shortname
102: }
103: 
104: func (g *GenericLinkNode) GetEndpoints() []Endpoint {
105: 	return g.endpoints
106: }
107: 
108: func (*GenericLinkNode) GetState() clabnodesstate.NodeState {
109: 	// The GenericLinkNode is the basis for Mgmt-Bridge and Host fake node.
110: 	// Both of these do generally exist. Hence the Deployed state in generally returned
111: 	return clabnodesstate.Deployed
112: }
113: 
114: func (g *GenericLinkNode) Delete(ctx context.Context) error {
115: 	for _, e := range g.endpoints {
116: 		var err error
117: 		if l := e.GetLink(); l != nil {
118: 			err = l.Remove(ctx)
119: 		} else {
120: 			err = e.Remove(ctx)
121: 		}
122: 		if err != nil {
123: 			return err
124: 		}
125: 	}
126: 	return nil
127: }

append(g.endpoints, e)\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (g *GenericLinkNode) AdoptEndpoint(e Endpoint) error {\n\tif e == nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"node %q cannot adopt a nil endpoint\", g.shortname)\n\t}\n\n\towner := e.GetNode()\n\tif owner == nil {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\"node %q cannot adopt endpoint %q without an owner\",\n\t\t\tg.shortname,\n\t\t\te.GetIfaceName(),\n\t\t)\n\t}\n\n\tif owner.GetShortName() != g.shortname {\n\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\"node %q cannot adopt endpoint %q owned by owned.GetIfaceName() == e.GetIfaceName() {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"node %q already tracks interface %q\", g.shortname, e.GetIfaceName())\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\n\tg.endpoints = append(g.endpoints, e)\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (g *GenericLinkNode) ReleaseEndpoint(e Endpoint) error {\n\tfor i, ep := range g.endpoints {\n\t\tif ep != e {\n\t\t\tcontinue\n\t\t}\n\n\t\tg.endpoints = append(g.endpoints[:i], g.endpoints[i+1:]...)\n\t\treturn nil\n\t}\n\n\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"node %q does not own endpoint %q\", g.shortname, e.GetIfaceName())\n}\n\nfunc (g *GenericLinkNode) GetShortName() string {\n\treturn g.shortname\n}\n\nfunc (g *GenericLinkNode) GetEndpoints() []Endpoint {\n\treturn g.endpoints\n}\n\nfunc (*GenericLinkNode) GetState() clabnodesstate.NodeState {\n\t// The GenericLinkNode is the basis for Mgmt-Bridge and Host fake node.\n\t// Both of these do generally exist. Hence the Deployed state in generally returned\n\treturn clabnodesstate.Deployed\n}\n\nfunc (g *GenericLinkNode) Delete(ctx context.Context) error {\n\tfor _, e := range g.endpoints {\n\t\tvar err error\n\t\tif l := e.GetLink(); l != nil {\n\t\t\terr = l.Remove(ctx)\n\t\t} else {\n\t\t\terr = e.Remove(ctx)\n\t\t}\n\t\tif err != nil {\n\t\t\treturn err\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}",

Reasoning
So `hostLinkNode.ExecFunction` runs in the root netns. If a user puts netem on an extended host link's node endpoint (or mgmt-net bridge endpoint), the code would apply it. The doc says only veth links are supported. For brief links there's an explicit guard; for extended links there is none (the schema adds netem to the shared endpoint definition, which I should verify). Let me check the schema definitions around lines 1180 and 1550.

Also check `LinkMgmtNetRaw` — its endpoint type is bridge (mgmt bridge node). Applying netem to mgmt bridge endpoint would work but contradict docs.

Now let me check the schema file around line 1186 (brief link) and 1565 (endpoint).
