Reasoning
So the schema adds `netem` to the shared `link-endpoint` definition, which is used by all extended link types (`link-type-veth`, `link-type-dummy`, `link-type-macvlan`?, `link-type-mgmt-net`?, `link-type-host`?, `link-type-vxlan`?). Let me check which extended link types use `link-endpoint`:

- `link-type-veth` → endpoints items `$ref: link-endpoint` ✓
- `link-type-dummy` → endpoint `$ref: link-endpoint` ✓
- `link-type-vxlan` → endpoint probably `$ref: link-endpoint`
- macvlan/host/mgmt-net have `endpoint` too.

So with extended `type: macvlan` / `type: host` / `type: mgmt-net` links, the schema allows `netem` on the endpoint but:
- For macvlan: NodeEndpoint gets netem → applyLinkImpairments would apply netem to the macvlan interface inside the node netns. That actually works technically (netem qdisc on macvlan device). Docs say veth only. Silently supported on macvlan? Or is it fine?
- For host links: netem would be applied in the root netns on the host interface — a mutation of host interface qdisc! Hmm — host links have HostInterface in root netns. If user sets netem on the node endpoint of a host link, netem gets applied to the node interface — fine. But if a user set netem... the host endpoint's Netem is nil (constructed directly). So only the node endpoint.
- For mgmt-net links: the node endpoint is a bridge endpoint attached to the mgmt bridge pseudo-node; netem on a bridge port (the container's interface) — the bridge endpoint's interface is the container's interface, actually the endpoint on the mgmt-net link is the container interface. Applying netem there in the container's netns — works.

But wait — which of these extended types have GetEndpoints that include the endpoint with netem? Let me check:
- LinkMacVlan.GetEndpoints: [HostEndpoint, NodeEndpoint]? Let me check.
- LinkVEth (host link resolves to LinkVEth): endpoints [ep, hostEp]. netem on ep (node endpoint) — applied.
- LinkMgmtNet: endpoints?

Now the important asymmetry: brief links reject netem for host/macvlan/mgmt-net, but extended links do not, and the doc says "Impairments are supported on veth links." However — behavior differences: For brief links with `host:` endpoint, netem is rejected with an error. For extended `type: host` links, netem on the node endpoint is accepted and applied in the node netns (works). For extended `type: macvlan`, netem on the node endpoint... LinkMacVlan's GetEndpoints? Let me check whether macvlan's endpoint would be part of c.Links endpoints — the NodeEndpoint is a real node endpoint, so it would be in node's endpoints and link endpoints. Netem applied to macvlan interface inside node netns — works technically. So extended macvlan silently gains support while brief macvlan is rejected. Inconsistent but not a crash. Hmm — actually wait, for brief links the check rejects; that's intentional per docs. For extended, no restriction. Is there an issue with the mgmt-net link? LinkMgmtNetRaw.Resolve: creates EndpointBridge for the node endpoint with the mgmt bridge node. Netem applied to that interface in container netns — works.

But hold on — the bigger asymmetry: extended `type: veth` links where an endpoint is `node: host`. E.g.:
```
- type: veth
  endpoints:
    - node: host
      interface: eth0
    - node: r1
      interface: eth1
```
Hmm, is that valid? In extended links, host endpoints are `node: host`? Let me check how the host node is resolved in extended links — `GetHostLinkNode()` is used for brief. For extended veth with `node: host`, the params.Nodes map contains "host"? Let me check how nodes map is built — there might be a pseudo "host" node registered. Let me grep.

Actually, let me focus: what happens if netem is set on an endpoint whose node is the mgmt-bridge or host pseudo-node? GenericLinkNode.ExecFunction works via nspath (root ns). applyEndpointImpairment would run in root ns and set a netem qdisc on e.g. the mgmt bridge's interface in the host root netns! That could be a real footgun: for a brief link the guard prevents `mgmt-net:br0` endpoint with netem. But for an extended `type: mgmt-net` link, the schema allows netem on the endpoint whose node is the mgmt bridge? No wait — mgmt-net link has one endpoint (the node) and the other side is the mgmt bridge defined by `mgmt-net` property. Let me check LinkMgmtNetRaw structure.

Let me also check the nodes map: does it include a "host" node? In containerlab, brief `host:` endpoints are handled via the special node name. For extended links `type: veth`, endpoint node must be an actual node in topology? Let me check EndpointRaw.Resolve — `params.Nodes[er.Node]` must exist. Is there a "host" entry in params.Nodes? Let me grep for how the nodes map is initialized with the host node.

Reasoning
So for the mgmt-net link, the endpoint is `contEp`, which is a node endpoint. If the user sets `netem` on the endpoint of an extended `type: mgmt-net` link, it would be applied to the container's interface — that's consistent-ish. The bridgeEp has nil Netem. OK.

For an extended veth link with an endpoint `node: host` (is that possible? params.Nodes contains "host" per core/clab.go line 901 `"host": clablinks.GetHostLinkNode()`). Yes! So an extended veth link can have `node: host` endpoint. If the user puts netem on the host endpoint (schema allows it — link-endpoint is shared), applyLinkImpairments would run in the ROOT netns and set a netem qdisc on an arbitrary host interface (e.g., eth0 on the host machine!). Wait — endpoint.GetIfaceName() for the host endpoint is the interface the user named. So `netem` on a `host:` endpoint of an extended veth link → apply netem qdisc to a host interface in the root namespace. The doc says impairments are supported on veth links (implying any veth link endpoint). But for brief links, host endpoints are explicitly rejected with "netem is only supported on veth links, not on host links". The inconsistency: brief rejects host endpoint netem, extended allows it — silently impairing a host interface in the root netns. That is a real inconsistency/footgun introduced by this PR: the guard exists only in `ToTypeSpecificRawLink` (brief path), not for extended links. Severity: P2/P3? Impact: user can accidentally impair host networking; inconsistent validation. The brief-link error message says netem only supported on veth links, but the same schema-valid config on an extended link is silently accepted. Also macvlan/mgmt-net brief rejection vs extended acceptance.

Hmm wait, actually for brief links, can an endpoint be `host:` inside a brief veth link? `parseLinkType("host")` → LinkTypeHost → hostLinkFromBrief — the brief form is type-agnostic; the type is determined by the special endpoint. So a brief link `["r1:eth1", "host:eth0"]` IS a host link, not veth. Right — brief links with host endpoints are host links by definition. So the netem rejection for host brief links is correct: "not on host links". And the doc statement "Impairments are supported on veth links" — for extended links, `type: veth` with `node: host` endpoint — is that a thing? Let me check whether extended veth links support host endpoints... `LinkVEthRaw.Resolve` calls `epr.Resolve(params, l)` which uses node.GetLinkEndpointType() → host → NewEndpointHost. So yes, extended veth links support host endpoints (that's the documented extended form of host links? Actually docs say host links are `type: host` with `host-interface`. But `node: host` in extended veth also works I think).

So: schema allows netem on any `link-endpoint` (including within `link-type-host`? let me check — link-type-host has `endpoint` $ref link-endpoint). So a user could set netem in `type: host` link's endpoint — applied to the node's interface in node netns — fine.

The real risk: netem on the `host` node's endpoint (extended veth) → qdisc on host root-ns interface. Is that harmful? It modifies host networking state. It's opt-in and arguably even a feature (impair host-side of the veth). Hmm, it's symmetric with `tools netem set -n <host?...>`. Actually `tools netem set` requires a node name from the lab; can it target the host? Let me check tools_netem.go. If tools netem can't target host, then topology netem applying to host is inconsistent.

Let me not overweight this. The primary observable inconsistency: brief link `["r1:eth1", "mgmt-net:br0"]` with netem → error; extended `type: mgmt-net` with netem on endpoint → silently applied to container interface (works fine actually). And extended `type: macvlan` with netem → applied to macvlan interface. These actually work; only host endpoints are the risky case. I'd report as P3: validation asymmetry — netem accepted on non-veth extended links (schema + code) while brief links and docs claim veth-only; most concerning for `node: host` endpoints where the qdisc lands in the root netns.

Hmm, wait. Let me reconsider: is it harmful or just inconsistent? The docs say "Impairments are supported on veth links." For extended veth with host endpoint — it IS a veth link. So applying netem to the host side of a veth pair in root netns is arguably "supported on veth links" as documented. Not a defect per se. But the brief-vs-extended asymmetry: brief rejects `host:` because that brief link is classified as a "host link" type. So `["r1:eth1", "host:eth0"]` with netem errors, while `type: veth` with `node: host` endpoint + netem works. Inconsistent UX but each defensible. I'll note it as P4/P3 documentation/consistency. Let me think about what's most actionable:

Actually, more actionable: the schema `link-endpoint.netem` applies to all link types (dummy, macvlan, mgmt-net, host, vxlan), while code+docs say veth only. For dummy links: LinkDummyRaw has one endpoint; netem on it → applied. That's fine-ish. For vxlan: EndpointVxlan — GetNode? netem on vxlan endpoint... The vxlan endpoint's node is the node; netem applied to the vxlan interface? Let me check EndpointVxlan and LinkVxlan GetEndpoints.

Let me check link_vxlan.go GetEndpoints and endpoint_vxlan.go — does EndpointVxlan implement GetNetem? It embeds EndpointGeneric? Let me check.

Also need to check `Endpoint` implementations all provide GetNetem (interface method added). Check endpoint_bridge.go, endpoint_host.go, endpoint_veth.go, endpoint_macvlan.go, endpoint_vxlan.go, endpoint_dummy.go, endpoint_runtime.go — all embed EndpointGeneric? Let me check endpoint_runtime.go — RuntimeEndpoint wrapper — does it delegate GetNetem? applyLinkImpairments uses endpoints from c.Links GetEndpoints(); in the restart path, `n.GetEndpoints()` may return RuntimeEndpoints. Let me check endpoint_runtime.go — it wraps an Endpoint; does it forward GetNetem? If not, compile error would occur... it must implement the interface. Let me check.

- Verified facts so far:
  - `core/start.go`/`core/restart.go`: apply impairments per node only on the non-labruntime path; `startNodesWithLabRuntime`/`restartNodesWithLabRuntime` (k8s labruntime) do NOT apply impairments — potential coverage gap.
  - `links/link_brief.go` `ToTypeSpecificRawLink` rejects netem on brief host/macvlan/mgmt-net links (specialLinkTypes check inside endpoint loop), but extended forms (`type: host`, `type: macvlan`, `type: mgmt-net`, possibly others) appear to allow netem via shared `EndpointRaw` (`Netem *NetemRaw` yaml tag) — `LinkMacVlanRaw.Resolve` calls `r.Endpoint.Resolve(params, link)` so netem would be set/applied on non-veth endpoints, contradicting docs "Impairments are supported on veth links".
  - `GenericLinkNode.ExecFunction` does `ns.GetNS(g.nspath)` + `netns.Do(f)` — applies in root ns for host/bridge pseudo-nodes.
  - veth-stitch: `NewVEthStitchedRawFromVEth` carries over `Endpoints` (including Netem), `LinkVEthRaw.Resolve` redirects for SR-SIM etc. — netem likely propagates to node endpoints (probably OK).
  - Brief links: netem assignment logic in `linkVEthRawFromLinkBriefRaw` (≤2 netem entries); `ToLinkBriefRaw` on `LinkVEthRaw` appends netem list only if some endpoint has netem.
  - `netem/netem.go` unchanged: `NewTC(ns)`, `SetImpairments` (calls `ReplaceImpairments`, then finds qdisc), `ReplaceImpairments`, `DeleteImpairments`; `init()` calls `core.InitializeClock()`. Callers: `core/link_netem.go` uses `ReplaceImpairments`; `cmd/tools_netem.go` uses `SetImpairments`/`DeleteImpairments`.
  - Schema: `netem` ref added at lines ~1186 (brief link netem array) and ~1565 (`netem` definition in shared `link-endpoint`-like definition, description "Link impairment ... like containerlab tools netem set") — need to confirm which link-type definitions share that endpoint definition (whether non-veth extended types also accept netem).
  - `links/netem.go` validation: `maxNetemJitter = math.MaxInt32 * time.Nanosecond`, `maxNetemRate = math.MaxUint64/1000`; jitter-without-delay error, loss 0–100 (NaN caught), rate overflow, corruption 0–100.
  - `links/netem_test.go` table-driven `TestNetemRawResolve` covers errors incl. "netem jitter must be at most 2.147483647s".
